\chapter{RE-017 --- Byte-Pipeline Recovery}\label{record-422}

\noindent{\footnotesize\color{BookMuted}\path{docs/perf/RE-017-byte-pipeline.md}\par}\smallskip

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Status:} In progress (audit phase). \textbf{Goal:} Recover the render pipeline to pre-RE-008 performance or better by eliminating all per-frame allocation and string work on the render path, moving to a pure byte-level output pipeline. \textbf{Cycle ID note:} the \texttt{b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e} backlog already has an \texttt{R\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}d} file using the same cycle number. These are separate concerns and we may need to renumber one of them before landing this cycle.
\end{quote}

\section{Background}\label{record-422-background}

After RE-008 merged (byte-packed surface representation, PR \#62, merge commit \texttt{0\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}3\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}7}), an audit of the existing renderer benchmark suite revealed a broad performance regression across every scenario:

The most dramatic finding: \textbf{\texttt{s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m} transient heap allocation grew from 1.4 MiB to 62 MiB per scenario run (44× increase)}. This is the signature of the current \texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d} path: \texttt{s\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e} Map growth, \texttt{S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e} key allocation per cell, \texttt{b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}+\allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.} string concatenation, and side-table grapheme string churn.

The original RE-017 plan was scoped to fix the gradient stress worst-case (\texttt{e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t} mode 1, which had regressed from 49 → 25 FPS). The audit revealed that the regression is much broader: every non-noop scenario is slower, and the heap allocation on the styled diff path is catastrophic. The byte-pipeline rewrite is now targeting the full regression, not just the gradient case.

An earlier claim of ``-5.5\% DOGFOOD'' from the RE-008 handoff does not reconcile with these bench numbers. Its origin is unknown. It may have measured the live \texttt{n\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d} experience rather than the headless bench, or it may have compared against a different reference. It is not being used as a success criterion for RE-017.

Per scenario (aggregated across samples), only \textbf{median} is currently computed. This is a known gap --- see \href{https://github.com/flyingrobots/bijou/blob/cbd70612140e7a450d764b3c7be1c6af878b99bc/docs/design}{RE-017 task I-2} for the expansion to mean / stddev / min / max / p50 / p90 / p99.

\subsection{Current limitations}\label{record-422-current-limitations}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{No percentile statistics.} Only median across samples. A bimodal distribution (e.g.~one slow sample every 10) would be invisible.
\item
  \textbf{No per-stage breakdown.} The \texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r} kind covers layout + paint as a unit; the \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f} kind adds the diff stage on top. We can see ``total'' cost but not ``how much in layout vs paint vs diff''.
\item
  \textbf{No conversion-hot-path counters.} The bench cannot answer ``how many \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x} calls per frame?'' or ``how many \texttt{S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e} key builds per frame?'' --- both suspected sources of the RE-008 slowdown.
\item
  \textbf{No GC pause time.} Only heap deltas. We can see how much allocation happened, but not whether it triggered a costly GC.
\item
  \textbf{First-run variance.} JIT warmup may be incomplete even after the scenario's \texttt{w\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s} --- the 10-run expanded audit is partly to characterize this.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m} uses the legacy \texttt{s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}\{\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{})} API, not \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B}.} The underlying surface is a \texttt{P\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e} either way, but the paint code path going into the diff is the hex-string-parsing path, not the byte-passing fast path. A \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} variant should be added.
\item
  \textbf{No gradient / truecolor stress scenario.} The \texttt{e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t} worst case (every cell a unique RGB pair, 9168+ unique styles per frame) is not represented in the headless bench. It is the scenario that originally surfaced the \texttt{s\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e} worst-case regression (49 → 25 FPS).
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{2026-04-09 --- Audit kickoff, 10×10 bench run launched}\label{record-422-audit-kickoff-1010-bench-run-launched}

Completed task I-0 (bench infrastructure audit). Key findings:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  The existing baseline at \texttt{b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n} was captured at commit \texttt{8\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}4\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}7} --- \textbf{before} the RE-008 merge (\texttt{0\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}3\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}7}). It represents the old cell-object path, not the byte-packed path we're optimizing. It is effectively a historical reference, not the RE-017 starting point.
\item
  A single run of the existing bench at HEAD (\texttt{9\allowbreak{}4\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}6\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}c}) against the stale baseline showed 12-53\% regressions across every non-noop scenario, and a 44× transient heap allocation jump (1.4 MiB → 62 MiB) in \texttt{s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m}. These numbers are from one run and have not yet been confirmed for stability.
\item
  To confirm stability, launched a 10-process × 10-samples-each expanded stats run (100 samples per scenario total), saving each process output to \texttt{b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}N\allowbreak{}N\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}. Task I-0b tracks this.
\item
  Created new audit tasks: I-0c (methodology docs), I-0d (pipeline instrumentation), I-0e (investigate broad slowdown).
\item
  Hypothesis to test in I-0e: per-cell hex string parsing in the \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} path is the bulk of the regression. Trace: \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{}5\allowbreak{}3\allowbreak{}2} → \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f} (line 300) → \texttt{i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} (line 288) + \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r} + \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}M\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s}. Pre-RE-008, \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} was an object assignment. Post-RE-008, every \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} call does 6 \texttt{c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}t}s, 6 \texttt{h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}D} lookups, modifier array iteration, and two byte writes per color. For a paint-heavy scenario that calls \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} per cell, this adds \textasciitilde20-30 operations per cell on a path that used to be a single object assignment. No compensating savings on \texttt{s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m} because it never enters the diff path where the byte-packed representation pays off.
\end{enumerate}

If the hypothesis holds, a cheap early win is possible: cache theme-token hex parses so repeated calls to \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}\{\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{})} reuse pre-parsed bytes instead of re-parsing on every call. This would be independent of the byte-pipeline rewrite.

\textbf{Key observations}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{The regression is reproducible and large.} Run-to-run variance is tight (CoV \textless{} 5\% for most scenarios). The 10 independent processes agree with each other. These are trustworthy numbers.
\item
  \textbf{The regression is bigger than the single-run showed.} Median comparison now gives 30\%-113\% regression, not 12-53\%. Some scenarios literally doubled in frame time (layout.normalize.medium +113\%, dogfood.render.large +106\%, dogfood.diff.large +98\%).
\item
  \textbf{Smaller scenarios regressed harder.} Scenarios with smaller baselines (layout.normalize 0.16ms, surface.paint 0.22ms, dogfood.render.large 0.53ms) regressed the most in percentage terms. This is consistent with per-cell overhead dominating when there's less fixed work to amortize against.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m} heap allocation is catastrophic and confirmed at \textasciitilde46× baseline.} Mean 51.79 MiB, P50 60.99 MiB, very stable (min 30 MiB, max 61 MiB). The differ's \texttt{s\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e} + string concat + side table churn is the culprit --- exactly what RE-017 is rewriting.
\item
  \textbf{Two scenarios are bimodal} (dogfood.render.medium CoV 25\%, styled.diff.medium CoV 16\%). Long right tails with max values 3-3.5× the P50. This is the GC pause signature: most samples are fast, occasional samples catch a generational GC triggered by the high allocation rate. Visible GC jank.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m} is unaffected.} The control scenario (full runtime loop with trivial view, no real rendering) matches baseline. This confirms the regression is specifically in the render/paint/diff/layout path, not in runtime/event loop overhead.
\item
  \textbf{The bimodal tail on \texttt{d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m} is the concerning one for end users} --- occasional 8ms frames on a 2ms mean means stutters during interaction, even though averages look OK.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Hypothesis status}

The per-cell hex parsing hypothesis is consistent with every pattern observed: - Scenarios that set every cell (layout.normalize, surface.paint, dogfood.render) regress hardest. - Smaller scenarios regress harder (per-cell overhead dominates). - Heap allocation on styled-diff is the sgrCache + string concat side of the same story (diff path, not paint path). - The control scenario is unaffected.

But it's still a hypothesis. Confirming requires instrumentation (task I-0d) to count parseHex / encodeChar / encodeModifiers calls per frame and measure time spent in each.

\textbf{Open questions}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Why does \texttt{l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}z\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m} regress so hard (+113\%) when it uses pre-built pattern surfaces? The setup cost of building those surfaces is outside the timed region, so hex parsing during setup shouldn't show. Does the blit operation in the packed path do extra work? Needs investigation. Possible explanation: blit copying from a legacy cell-array surface to a packed surface (or vice versa) forces per-cell re-encoding.
\item
  Why does \texttt{f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m} regress only 30\% when it's structurally similar to the dogfood.render paths? Is the frame shell short-circuiting some rendering?
\item
  Why does \texttt{d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m} (+17\%) regress much less than \texttt{d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m} (+45\%) despite both using the docs app? What's different about the keyed explorer state?
\end{itemize}

These questions go into task I-0e (investigate).

\textbf{Next actions}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Task I-0b: COMPLETE.
\item
  Task I-0e (investigate RE-008 broad slowdown): unblocked, can start now. Priority: identify the specific hot paths causing the regression using bisection across the RE-008 commits if needed.
\item
  Task I-0d (instrument pipeline): can start in parallel with I-0e; the instrumentation will accelerate the investigation.
\item
  Task I-0c (document methodology): largely already done as part of the scaffold + this log entry; needs a final pass to confirm nothing is missing.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Deferred decisions}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Whether to refresh \texttt{b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n} to reflect the post-RE-008 state as the new RE-017 starting point (task I-9). Leaning yes, but keeping the \texttt{8\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}4\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}7} baseline as a historical reference for the ultimate ``did we recover?'' question at end of cycle.
\item
  Whether the gradient stress scenario (task I-5) is still a priority given the existing bench already exposes the regression. Still yes --- the gradient case is the worst-case stress and we want it in the CI gate.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{2026-04-09 --- Scrapped the existing bench, built bench v2 from scratch}\label{record-422-scrapped-the-existing-bench-built-bench-v2-from-scratch}

After the expanded 10×10 audit revealed that the \texttt{P\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}O\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}'\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}'\allowbreak{})}-based heap measurement fix produced garbage data (zero GC events reported across 1,000 samples even for scenarios with negative heap deltas, which can only happen if GC fires), we made the call to throw out \texttt{s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}*\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s} + \texttt{b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n} entirely and build a new bench from scratch around the principle ``measure only what you can trust.''

\textbf{What was deleted:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s}
\item
  \texttt{s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s}
\item
  \texttt{s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s}
\item
  \texttt{s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s}
\item
  \texttt{s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s}
\item
  \texttt{b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/} (the whole directory, including the 10 audit runs)
\item
  \texttt{n\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}*} scripts from the root package.json
\end{itemize}

\textbf{What was built:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}/} --- new top-level workspace package \texttt{@\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h}
\item
  Harness-agnostic \texttt{S\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o} interface in \texttt{b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s}
\item
  4 initial scenarios:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}i} --- baseline ``no colors, no hex parsing''
  \item
    \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e} --- rotating theme palette, exercises \texttt{i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B}
  \item
    \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}b} --- per-cell unique RGB via \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B}
  \item
    \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t} --- full-screen gradient diff (sgrCache stress)
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \texttt{b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}/} --- process-isolated wall-time harness

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \texttt{c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s} runs ONE sample of ONE scenario in a fresh V8 isolate and exits
  \item
    \texttt{r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s} spawns N children per scenario, aggregates stats
  \item
    \texttt{c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s} diffs two report JSONs with a regression gate
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \texttt{b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s} --- percentile/mean/stddev/CoV helpers (linear-interpolated)
\item
  \texttt{b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s} --- \texttt{b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}|\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}|\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t}
\item
  \texttt{b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}M\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}d} --- design principles and usage
\item
  \texttt{b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}4\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}6\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n} --- first trustworthy baseline
\end{itemize}

\textbf{New npm scripts in root package.json:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{n\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h} → \texttt{n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n}
\item
  \texttt{n\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e}
\item
  \texttt{n\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Design principles documented in \texttt{b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}M\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}d}:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Process isolation per sample. Fresh V8 isolate every time. No cross-sample GC state. No accumulated JIT state. The scenario's own warmup loop stabilizes JIT within each child.
\item
  Wall time only in the main bench. No heap deltas, no inline GC observers, no anything that can be wrong. \texttt{p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} start + end, divide by frame count, done.
\item
  Scenarios are pure: \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} + \texttt{f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} + metadata. No coupling to measurement. The same module can drive the wall-time harness, a future memory harness, and a future interactive sandbox --- three harnesses sharing one scenario registry.
\item
  Report stats, not means. mean / stddev / CoV / min / max / p50 / p90 / p99. CoV \textless{} 5\% is the ``trust this'' signal.
\end{enumerate}

}

30 samples per scenario. \textbf{All CoVs under 4\%.} These are trustworthy numbers for the first time in this cycle. The bench is reproducible and the new sample runs take \textasciitilde45 seconds total, which makes iterative optimization measurement practical.

\textbf{Per-cell cost breakdown (for context, rough):}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}i}: 234 µs / 12,760 cells ≈ \textbf{18 ns/cell} (baseline)
\item
  \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e}: 634 µs / 12,760 cells ≈ \textbf{50 ns/cell} (2.7× ASCII cost --- the delta is hex parsing + modifier encoding per set() call)
\item
  \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}b}: 946 µs / 12,760 cells ≈ \textbf{74 ns/cell} (4.0× ASCII cost --- BUT this scenario's cost is dominated by 6 \texttt{M\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s} calls per cell for animation, not by \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} itself. Scenario design note below.)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Scenario design concern --- \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}b} is animation-bound:}

The scenario spends most of its per-cell time on \texttt{M\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s} × 6 for the animation phase, not on \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B}. This means it does not cleanly isolate the \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} path cost. To fix: add a variant that precomputes a large RGB table in \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} and indexes into it in \texttt{f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} with cheap arithmetic. That would give us a pure ``\texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} vs \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t}'' comparison. Captured as TODO --- not blocking the RE-017 investigation since the scenario still exposes the sgrCache stress case in \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t}.

\textbf{Backlog entries captured this session (from observations during the audit):}

Bad-code:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}8\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}d} --- the hex-string-as-canonical-fg/bg smell. The broad RE-008 regression's likely root cause.
\item
  \texttt{d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}W\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}z\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}d} --- the \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}W\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}s} bitmap in \texttt{s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s} serves the lazy Cell{[}{]} cache, not render tracking. Blocks the Task II-1 repurposing until the lazy cache is retired.
\item
  \texttt{d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}d} --- the DAG renderer occludes layer-skipping edges. Correctness bug (git-warp consumer). Not blocking RE-017 but important.
\item
  \texttt{d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}X\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}8\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}d} --- \texttt{P\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}O\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}'\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}'\allowbreak{})} didn't report events it should have. Do not use it as a measurement filter in bijou benches until someone reproduces it in isolation.
\end{itemize}

  \texttt{d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}d} --- \texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f} branded type. Follow-on to the theme cache, makes the fast path first-class in the type system.
\item
  \texttt{d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}X\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}6\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}d} --- tag scenarios for intelligent filtering. Enables targeted CI gates and coverage analysis.

  \texttt{d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}X\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}d} --- JSONL flat-record output format for agent consumption. An agent-first observation from this session: I work faster when tools emit grep/jq-friendly data.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Agent-first lessons from this audit}

(This section is the agent advocating for what the agent needs.)

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Measurement integrity is the first thing to validate, not the last.} I spent real time building on top of a broken measurement before questioning the tool. When numbers look weird (negative heap deltas, zero GC events), that's a tool problem until proven otherwise.
\item
  \textbf{Claims I make about code behavior need to be backed by a citation or a minimal repro.} I claimed the DAG renderer hid edges via transitive reduction. It didn't. I claimed a 5.5\% DOGFOOD regression existed. It might not. Both cost trust.
\item
  \textbf{Append-only discipline on running logs is a real requirement, not an aesthetic preference.} I had to be told this. The memory is saved.
\item
  \textbf{The backlog lanes (\texttt{b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}/}, \texttt{c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/}) are my tools too, not just the user's.} I am a co-author of the project's self-knowledge. When I notice smells or have ideas I should log them immediately, not wait to be asked. This session flipped that for me.
\end{enumerate}

Direct way to measure: bisect across the RE-008 commits with \texttt{b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e}.

\subsection{2026-04-09 --- Investigation result: RE-018 hex-parse hypothesis CONFIRMED}\label{record-422-investigation-result-re-018-hex-parse-hypothesis-confirmed}

Approach: source-diff the pre-RE-008 (\texttt{8\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}4\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}7}) \texttt{s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s} against current HEAD, then add a minimal A/B/C bench scenario to quantify the cost of each path empirically.

\textbf{Code review --- pre vs post RE-008 \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} path}

Pre-RE-008 \texttt{s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}7} (\texttt{g\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}8\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}4\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}7\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s}):

\begin{CodeBlock}
set(x, y, cell, mask = FULL_MASK) {
  if (x < 0 || x >= w || y < 0 || y >= h) return;
  const idx = y * w + x;
  applyMaskInPlace(cells[idx]!, cell, mask);
},
\end{CodeBlock}

Pre-RE-008 \texttt{a\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}M\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e} (line 231): five property assignments. \texttt{t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}g} was a string reference copy. No parsing. No encoding. Total per-call work: bounds check + array index + \textasciitilde5 property assignments. Cheap.

Post-RE-008 \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} (current \texttt{s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{}5\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}3\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}5\allowbreak{}4\allowbreak{}0}) routes through \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f} (line 300), which does on every call:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{})} --- length check + \texttt{c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}t} or sideTable lookup
\item
  \texttt{i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}+\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{})} --- 6× \texttt{c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}t} + 6× \texttt{h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}D} (lookup table) + 3 byte writes
\item
  \texttt{i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}+\allowbreak{}5\allowbreak{})} --- same, 6 more charCodeAt
\item
  \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}M\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{})} --- array iteration + 2 record lookups per modifier
\item
  10 total byte writes into the packed buffer
\item
  \texttt{m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{})} --- bit flip
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Per-call cost blowup:} \textasciitilde5 operations → \textasciitilde40-60 operations. 8-12× more work per \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} call, depending on whether fg/bg/modifiers are present.

\textbf{A/B/C bench --- three scenarios on the same painting}

Added \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d} as the ``no hex parse, no math'' baseline. Same 12,760-cell paint, but via \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}O\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}K\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}3\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{})} --- pre-parsed bytes, no string work at all.

\texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d} & {1\allowbreak{}4\allowbreak{}3} {µ\allowbreak{}s} & \textbf{{1\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}2} {n\allowbreak{}s}} & \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} {→} \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l} {d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t} {(\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s} {i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{},} {b\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s} {o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{})} \\
\texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}i} & {2\allowbreak{}3\allowbreak{}1} {µ\allowbreak{}s} & \textbf{{1\allowbreak{}8\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}1} {n\allowbreak{}s}} & \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}\{\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{})} {→} \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f} {(\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o} {c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{},} {b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t} {a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l} {b\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s} {s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l} {e\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{})} \\
\texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e} & {6\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}5} {µ\allowbreak{}s} & \textbf{{4\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}0} {n\allowbreak{}s}} & \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}\{\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{})} {→} \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f} {→} {2\allowbreak{}×} \texttt{i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} \\
\end{longtable}
}

\textbf{The deltas tell the story:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e} − \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d} = 482 µs per frame of pure hex-parsing + modifier-encoding overhead.} That is 38 ns × 12,760 cells of work that is \emph{entirely} spent reparsing the same \textasciitilde5 theme colors over and over. This is \textbf{77\% of \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e}'s total frame time}.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}i} − \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d} = 88 µs / 7 ns/cell.} This is the raw cost of \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f}'s function overhead + branch-check path even when there's no hex to parse. Not huge, but real.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} is unambiguously the fast path.} It writes MORE data per call (10 bytes vs \textasciitilde2-3 bytes of char code) but is \textasciitilde1.6× faster than \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}\{\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{})} because it skips \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f} and hits \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l} directly.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Conclusion}

The RE-018 hypothesis is confirmed. The broad RE-008 regression (30-113\% across every rendering scenario in the earlier audit) is driven by per-cell hex parsing in the legacy \texttt{s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t} path, which is what \textasciitilde every component currently uses. \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} exists and works, but adoption is partial --- most components still pass hex strings.

\textbf{Two independent fixes are implied, and they compose:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Cheap, no-API-break win:} the cool idea \texttt{R\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}d}. Pre-parse theme token colors at theme load time into byte arrays. \texttt{s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t} (or a new internal variant) detects already-parsed byte values and skips \texttt{i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B}. Components that pull fg/bg from theme tokens (which is the common case) get the fast path for free. Expected impact: \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e} drops from 625 µs toward 143 µs --- potentially a \textbf{4×} speedup on that scenario and roughly proportional speedups on \texttt{d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}*}.
\item
  \textbf{Deeper fix:} the cool idea \texttt{R\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}d}. A branded \texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f} type that lets the entire hot path work in bytes. Hex strings stay supported at the user edge. Components that build Cells directly can \texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{})} at setup time and reuse the result.
\end{enumerate}

The theme cache (RE-019 cool idea) is the right first landing because it's:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Zero API break --- purely internal optimization.
\item
  Directly measurable --- \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e} delta is the success criterion.
\item
  Compounds with the byte-pipeline differ work in Part II --- if we land both, the entire render pipeline runs in bytes end to end.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Also worth noting for the byte-pipeline design}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} not being \textasciitilde identical in cost to \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}\{\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{})} is a design assertion we can make now.} \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} is the fast path; components should use it wherever they have the RGB values already. Converting more components to \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} would be an early partial win even without any new caching.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}i} being 1.6× slower than \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d} suggests there is room to optimize \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f}'s no-colors path.} Currently the branches are evaluated but never taken. A fast-path version that detects ``no fg, no bg, no modifiers'' at the top and jumps to a minimal char-only writer would recover most of that 88 µs delta. Not blocking, but an obvious cleanup for the same cycle.
\end{itemize}

If it matches expectations (3-4× speedup), land it as a standalone improvement before the Part II byte-pipeline work.

\subsection{2026-04-09 --- Theme token color cache prototype: 1.9× speedup confirmed}\label{record-422-theme-token-color-cache-prototype-1.9-speedup-confirmed}

Prototyped RE-019 cool idea (pre-parse theme token colors into bytes). Scope of the change:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l} interface gained optional \texttt{f\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}?\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}[\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}]} and \texttt{b\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}?\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}[\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}]} fields (\texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}5\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}3\allowbreak{}8}). Both are optional; hex strings still work as before. No breaking change.
\item
  \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f} (\texttt{s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{}3\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}3\allowbreak{}4\allowbreak{}8}) now checks \texttt{f\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} / \texttt{b\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} first and uses them directly when present, falling back to \texttt{i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{})} otherwise. Theme-driven paints that pass pre-parsed RGB skip the hex parse entirely.
\item
  \texttt{C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e} in \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s} extended to \texttt{P\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}<\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}'\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}'\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}|\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}'\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}'\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}|\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}'\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}'\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}|\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}'\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}'\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}|\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}'\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}'\allowbreak{}>}.
\item
  \texttt{t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e} (the central helper used by text components) now passes \texttt{t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} / \texttt{t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} through when present. Since theme resolution already populates these fields (see \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}2}), any component that uses \texttt{t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e} gets the fast path automatically.
\item
  \texttt{b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s} updated to pass \texttt{b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} explicitly as a representative component migration.
\end{enumerate}

}

\textbf{Result: \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e} → \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e} = 624 µs → 328 µs, a 1.9× speedup.} That's a \textbf{47\% reduction in frame time} on the theme-driven paint path with a \textasciitilde30-line change.

The remaining gap to the \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} floor (328 → 145 µs) is other overhead in \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f} that has nothing to do with hex parsing:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r} --- char string → code point encoding
\item
  \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}M\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s} --- array iteration + record lookups
\item
  Undefined-check branches for cell fields
\item
  Object property accesses on the Cell argument
\item
  Writing zero bytes to unused fg/bg positions when they're absent
\end{itemize}

Those are a separate optimization opportunity (an \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t} sibling that takes pre-computed byte/flag values directly), not part of the theme cache work.

\textbf{What this means for real-world performance}

Theme tokens already had \texttt{f\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B}/\texttt{b\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} populated via \texttt{p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B} (the infrastructure was half-built from an earlier cycle and never wired through). Every component that uses \texttt{t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e} now gets the \textasciitilde1.9× speedup for free because \texttt{t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e} passes the pre-parsed RGB through. That includes everything built on \texttt{c\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e} and \texttt{s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s}'s text rendering helpers --- the hottest text paint path in the system.

Components that still call \texttt{s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}\{\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{})} directly (without going through \texttt{t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e}) don't benefit yet. They need a similar small edit to pass \texttt{f\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B}. The grep turned up \textasciitilde8 files with direct \texttt{.\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x} calls. Migrating them is mechanical and safe.
