All CoVs under 8\% (paint-rgb-fixed's 7.4\% is the absolute-floor effect; 145 µs is tight enough that OS jitter dominates). All others under 5\%. Numbers are trustworthy.

\textbf{Targets for Part II}

Baseline numbers give us concrete Part II targets:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c}: 227 µs → \textasciitilde0 µs} (II-1 render-dirty bitmap should skip the scan entirely on an unchanged surface). This is the sharpest measurable win target in Part II.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t}: 2.08 ms → \textless1.5 ms} (II-4 byte pipeline should drop the sgrCache overhead and string concat garbage).
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c}: 469 µs → \textless350 µs} (combined win from II-1 dirty-bit skip + II-4 byte emission).
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e}: 349 µs → \textless200 µs} (II-1 should skip the 90\% unchanged cells; II-4 should cheapen the 10\% emit).
\end{enumerate}

Any Part II step that doesn't move at least one of these needles while matching all the others isn't worth shipping.

\textbf{Next: Phase B --- Part II prereqs}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{II-2: \texttt{W\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s} API} --- small interface addition. Can run in parallel with II-1 conceptually; I'll do it first since it's isolated and small.
\item
  \textbf{II-1: Repurpose \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}W\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}s} as render-dirty bitmap} --- the big unlock. Requires auditing \texttt{s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} hot-path usage first (see bad-code RE-019). Then decide drop-cache vs separate-bitmap. Then implement + measure against \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c} and \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e}.
\end{enumerate}

Part II is bigger work than Phase A. Will likely need to split across multiple sessions.

\subsection{2026-04-10 --- II-2 + II-1 landed: 30× faster diff-static, 1.9× faster diff-sparse}\label{record-422-ii-2-ii-1-landed-30-faster-diff-static-1.9-faster-diff-sparse}

Two pieces of Part II shipped this session.

\textbf{II-2: \texttt{W\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s} API (task \#2)}

Added optional \texttt{w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}U\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}8\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{})} method to the \texttt{W\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t} interface (\texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s}). Implemented in \texttt{n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}O\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} (\texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s}) backed by \texttt{p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{})\allowbreak{})}. The \texttt{l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n} parameter is required so pooled buffers with extra capacity can write only the valid prefix without slicing.

This is plumbing for II-3 (pooled output buffer) and II-4 (differ direct byte emission). No functional change yet --- the differ still uses the string \texttt{w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} path. Tests pass, types clean.

\textbf{II-1: Render-dirty bitmap (task \#11)}

Implemented as a SECOND bitmap alongside the existing \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}W\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}s} (which stays for the lazy \texttt{C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}[\allowbreak{}]} decode cache). Decision rationale:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The Cell{[}{]} cache is still used by \texttt{s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}[\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}]} accessors (notably \texttt{g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s} middleware), and by tests. Dropping it would be a breaking API change requiring more migration.
\item
  Adding a second \texttt{U\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}3\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}y} is cheap (1 bit per cell, \textasciitilde1.6 KiB for a 220×58 surface).
\item
  Both bitmaps update from the same \texttt{m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{})} helper, so there's no risk of drift. The \texttt{m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} helper resets both. The \texttt{m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} helper sets both.
\end{itemize}

\texttt{P\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e} interface gained:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}W\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}U\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}3\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}y} --- exposed for the differ to read.
\item
  \texttt{m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d} --- public method for callers that model runtime swap manually (bench scenarios, advanced loops).
\end{itemize}

\texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d} (\texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{}4\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}1}) got two changes:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Whole-frame early-out}: at the top, scan the union of \texttt{t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}W\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}|\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}W\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}s} word-at-a- time. If no words have any bits set, return immediately without walking any cells. This is the diff-static / idle-frame case.
\item
  \textbf{Per-cell skip}: in the inner loop, before doing the byte compare, check if the current cell's bit is set in either surface's bitmap. If neither marks it dirty, skip it without the compare. This is the diff-sparse case.
\end{enumerate}

The bitmap gets set on every \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})}, \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})}, \texttt{f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})}, \texttt{b\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})}, \texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} mutation, and reset by \texttt{c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} or explicit \texttt{m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})}. The runtime model is: \texttt{n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} between frames resets the bitmap; user paints set bits; renderDiff walks union; swap.

The bench scenarios \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c}, \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e}, and \texttt{d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c} were updated to call \texttt{m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} between frames to model the runtime swap pattern. Without this modeling the bitmap accumulates over many frames and the win disappears in the bench (though it would still apply correctly in the real runtime).

Paint scenarios are neutral as expected --- they don't go through the diff path. \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t} and \texttt{d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c} are neutral because every cell is dirty (full repaint). \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e} (\textasciitilde10\% dirty) and \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c} (0\% dirty) are exactly the cases II-1 was designed to optimize, and they show the predicted wins.

\texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c}'s 28\% CoV is the only ``ugly'' number --- but it's the absolute-floor effect: at 7.58 µs, OS scheduler jitter dominates absolute variance. The number is real.

\textbf{Why dogfood-realistic didn't move}

\texttt{d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c} paints every cell in the surface every frame (it's the ``full repaint after scroll/resize'' worst case). Every cell is dirty, so the per-cell skip never fires. To benefit from II-1, a real DOGFOOD frame would need to only repaint changed regions --- which is exactly what real components do. The bench scenario is intentionally pessimistic.

\textbf{What's NOT yet optimized in II-1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The bench's runtime swap is modeled by an explicit \texttt{m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} call. The real runtime (\texttt{b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}i}'s \texttt{r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s}) does NOT yet call this. Currently the runtime relies on \texttt{n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} between frames, which already resets the bitmap (via the existing \texttt{m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} extension). Should be a no-op change to verify, but worth confirming.
\item
  Components that render directly into a long-lived surface without going through \texttt{c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} won't see the win until they start managing the bitmap explicitly.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Next: II-3 + II-4 (the byte pipeline)}

The differ still produces a per-frame \texttt{o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g} via \texttt{+\allowbreak{}=} concatenation, hits the \texttt{s\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e} Map for SGR codes, and calls \texttt{i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{})} at the end. Part II's main work is replacing all of that with byte-level emission into a pooled \texttt{U\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}8\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}y} and \texttt{i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{})}. Targets:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t}: 2.07 ms → \textless{} 1.5 ms (kill the sgrCache stress case)
\item
  \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}b} is unaffected (it's a paint-only scenario)
\item
  All other scenarios should remain neutral or improve modestly
\end{itemize}

II-3 and II-4 are bigger and probably need to split across sessions. Stopping here for this turn.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{2026-04-10 --- II-3 lands as opt-in plumbing (no perf delta)}\label{record-422-ii-3-lands-as-opt-in-plumbing-no-perf-delta}

\textbf{Goal}: pool a \texttt{U\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}8\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}y} in the TUI runtime and route the differ's output through \texttt{W\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s} instead of \texttt{i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{})}. Set up the API surface that II-4 will use for direct byte-level emission.

\textbf{What landed}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}?\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}U\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}8\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}y} field on the pipeline state.
\item
  \texttt{b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}i} runtime allocates a pooled buffer in \texttt{r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})}, resizes it in \texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})}, and threads it through to the default Diff middleware via \texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e}. Buffer is sized \texttt{c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}*\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}*\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}6\allowbreak{}4\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}+\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}4\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}6} (per-cell upper bound for ANSI + char

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    reset + cursor move with comfortable slack).
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}?\allowbreak{})} and \texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}?\allowbreak{})} both gain a trailing \texttt{o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f} parameter and forward it to the packed differ.
\item
  \texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d} keeps its existing string-based emission loop unchanged. At the very end, when \emph{all} of (caller passed an \texttt{o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f}, port supplies \texttt{w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s}, and the UTF-8-worst-case expansion of the composed string fits the buffer) are true, it encodes the frame once via \texttt{T\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o} and hands the bytes to \texttt{i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{})}. Otherwise it falls back to \texttt{i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{})} --- correctness before optimization.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The ``encodeInto on every small write'' detour}

The first attempt replaced every \texttt{o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}+\allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}X} site in the differ with a \texttt{w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}X\allowbreak{})} helper that called \texttt{T\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o} per chunk. Predicted neutral; measured \textbf{regression}:

Per-call \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o} setup overhead dominates when the source strings are short (cursor moves, SGR codes, single graphemes). The V8 string-concat ropes that the legacy path relied on are free by comparison.

Lesson: \textbf{the byte path's value is in \texttt{w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s} itself} --- specifically in handing a pre-encoded buffer to the OS instead of re-walking a JS string at the stream boundary. It is \textbf{not} in the act of encoding small chunks early. Until the differ can emit SGR + cursor bytes \emph{directly} without ever materializing them as JS strings (II-4's job), the cheapest correct shape is one encodeInto call per frame, on the fully composed output.

Reverted to the single-encodeInto-at-end design and re-measured --- neutral as expected.

The diff-static ``improvement'' is mostly the floor-noise effect (7.82 µs is well inside the 13.6\% CoV bin); the remaining scenarios are within ±3\% of baseline. Plumbing achieved without regression.

\textbf{Why bench scenarios stay on the string path}

The bench's counting sinks deliberately do not implement \texttt{w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s}. With the byte path gated on \texttt{o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}!\allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}\&\allowbreak{}\&\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}!\allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d}, all bench scenarios fall through to \texttt{i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{})} and measure the unchanged emission loop. The runtime alone opts in to the bytes path in production. This was a deliberate choice after the first attempt showed that letting the bench exercise the \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o}-at-end path adds work the bench sink does not need to do (it counts \texttt{t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h}, no real byte conversion).

When II-4 lands, both bench and runtime should be measurable on the byte path because there will no longer be a string intermediate to skip past.

\textbf{What II-4 has to do}

The differ still builds an \texttt{o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g} in the inner loop. II-4 replaces that with direct byte writes:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{m\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{})} becomes a byte writer that emits \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}[}, decimal y+1, \texttt{;}, decimal x+1, \texttt{H} directly into the pooled buffer.
\item
  \texttt{e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f} is replaced by a byte-level SGR builder. The \texttt{s\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e} Map and its \texttt{s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}K\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}y} String.fromCharCode indirection both go away --- there is no string to cache anymore.
\item
  Batch text becomes a \texttt{T\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{})} call against the pooled buffer (one per batch, not per cell).
\item
  \texttt{R\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}R} becomes a 4-byte literal write.
\end{enumerate}

Once that lands, \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t}'s 9168-entry sgrCache stress case should drop substantially. The current 2.02 ms is the ceiling we are trying to beat.

\textbf{Files touched (II-3)}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s} --- \texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f} and \texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d} gain optional \texttt{o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f}; encode-at-end byte path under feature gates; \texttt{t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r} module-level singleton.
\item
  \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s} --- \texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e} gains \texttt{o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f} and forwards.
\item
  \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s} --- \texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}?\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}U\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}8\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}y} field.
\item
  \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s} --- \texttt{a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}O\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{})} helper, ownership inside \texttt{r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})}, pass-through into the default Diff middleware and \texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e} constructor.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Smoke test}

\texttt{n\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d} (landing + docs) passes. Real production traffic now exercises the encode-at-end byte path; the bench shows no regression on the string-path scenarios it can see.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{2026-04-10 --- II-4 lands the byte-native differ (-23\% diff-gradient)}\label{record-422-ii-4-lands-the-byte-native-differ--23-diff-gradient}

\textbf{Goal}: replace the composed \texttt{o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g} inside \texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d} with direct byte writes into the pooled buffer. Drop the \texttt{s\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e}, \texttt{s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}K\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}y}, \texttt{b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r}, and \texttt{e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f} machinery entirely. Beat the old cached-string path on diff-gradient, which the handoff targeted at \textless{} 1.5 ms.

\textbf{What landed}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  New byte writers inside \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s}:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \texttt{w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{})} --- ASCII decimal for 0--999 values (cursor coordinates + RGB channels).
  \item
    \texttt{w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{})} --- \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}[\allowbreak{}Y\allowbreak{};\allowbreak{}X\allowbreak{}H} directly.
  \item
    \texttt{w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}O\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{})} --- full SGR prelude (reset + optional FG truecolor + optional BG truecolor + bold/dim/strike/inverse + underline variants) byte-identical to the legacy \texttt{b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{})} output.
  \item
    \texttt{w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}O\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{})} --- inlines ASCII / 2-byte BMP / 3-byte BMP UTF-8 encoding; falls back to \texttt{T\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o} only for side-table graphemes (multi-codepoint clusters).
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d} rewritten: the inner loop writes cursor + SGR + chars + trailing reset directly into \texttt{b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f} at \texttt{o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f}, and the function flushes via \texttt{i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{})} at the end (with a \texttt{T\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e} fallback when the sink lacks \texttt{w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s}, preserving the mock-sink test contract).
\item
  The \texttt{s\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e} / \texttt{s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}K\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}y} / \texttt{b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r} / \texttt{e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f} string-cache layer is gone. So are the \texttt{F\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}R} / \texttt{U\allowbreak{}N\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}N\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}R} / \texttt{D\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}R} / \texttt{R\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}R} string constants used only by \texttt{b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r}.
\item
  \texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}?\allowbreak{})} still has the II-3 public signature; the byte path now \emph{always} runs on \texttt{P\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e} regardless of whether the caller passes an \texttt{o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f}. Tests/bench without \texttt{o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f} hit a lazy module-level scratch buffer sized against the live surface.
\item
  \texttt{b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}i} runtime \texttt{O\allowbreak{}U\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}U\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}Y\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}L} bumped from 64 → 96 with \texttt{O\allowbreak{}U\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}U\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}K} 4096 → 8192, matching the differ's \texttt{S\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}Y\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}L} budget for worst-case-per-cell direct emission.
\item
  Bench counting sinks (\texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t}, \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e}, \texttt{d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c}, \texttt{d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c}) implement \texttt{w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s} again so they exercise the same code path as production Node stdout rather than the \texttt{T\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e} fallback.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The \texttt{b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}O\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}O\allowbreak{}N\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{})} detour}

First cut used pre-allocated \texttt{U\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}8\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}y} constants for every fixed byte sequence (\texttt{B\allowbreak{}Y\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}R}, \texttt{B\allowbreak{}Y\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}U\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}O\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}O\allowbreak{}R}, \texttt{B\allowbreak{}Y\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}O\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}D}, \ldots) and blitted them with \texttt{b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{})}. Clean code, but bench showed diff-gradient \emph{regressed} to 2.16 ms vs the 2.02 ms II-3 baseline --- still better than the uncached string build in theory, but worse in practice.

Root cause: \texttt{T\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t} has per-call setup cost that dominates when the source is tiny (4--7 bytes). The hot path was calling \texttt{b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t} \textasciitilde15× per cell × 9168 cells per frame. Inlining the sequences as individual \texttt{b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}[\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}]\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}…} writes:

Paint scenarios drift +2--3\% --- within CoV noise on most and probably attributable to a small JIT reshuffle from the new byte-writer symbol set. The wins are on the differ hot path, which is what Part II was designed to move.

vs the pre-RE017 baseline (\texttt{H\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}4\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}6\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}c}, the cycle start): diff-gradient 2.13 ms → 1.56 ms (−27\%). The hex-parse regression and the byte-pipeline recovery combined land the differ hot path meaningfully \emph{below} where it was when the cycle started.

\textbf{Why the \texttt{s\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e} ``stress'' was a misdiagnosis}

The cycle start assumed \texttt{s\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e} was hurting diff-gradient because the scenario's per-cell unique styles filled the cache with \textasciitilde9168 entries per frame that each got used once. The handoff targeted 2.07 ms → \textless{} 1.5 ms by ``killing the sgrCache stress''.

In practice, the cache was near-neutral at 2.02 ms --- V8's map hashing is fast enough that a high-miss-rate cache is just a small tax, not a dominant cost. The real wins came from the \emph{emission} rewrite: eliminating the per-frame string concat ladder (\texttt{o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}+\allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}+\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}+\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t} × many cells) and replacing it with direct byte writes into a pooled buffer. The byte-cache-vs-string-cache question turned out to be a distraction; what mattered was killing the string allocations on the hot path.

The handoff target of \textless{} 1.5 ms came close (1.56 ms p50, 1.51 µs p-min). The remaining gap would need an II-5-class micro-opt on top of the byte writer --- probably a 256-entry decimal byte table or inlining \texttt{w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s} into the per-cell loop. Deferring.

\textbf{Smoke test}

\texttt{n\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d} (landing + docs) passes. All 2807 tests pass --- the byte path is byte-identical to the old string path for every test scenario, so the mock-sink \texttt{T\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e} fallback produces the same strings the tests asserted against.

\textbf{What remains for Phase B}

II-5/6/7 micro-opts are still on the table: - II-5 ASCII fast-path char writer specialization - II-6 Pre-encoded side-table UTF-8 bytes (avoids \texttt{e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o} for repeated emoji graphemes) - II-7 Decimal lookup table for cursor/RGB (closes the remaining diff-gradient gap to the \textless{} 1.5 ms target)

Phase E ship work (III-1..6) is otherwise untouched.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{2026-04-10 --- II-5 hot-loop restructure (diff-gradient hits \textless{} 1.5 ms)}\label{record-422-ii-5-hot-loop-restructure-diff-gradient-hits-1.5-ms}

\textbf{Goal}: close the last \textasciitilde60 µs gap on diff-gradient (1.56 ms → \textless{} 1.5 ms target).

\textbf{The decimal-lookup-table detour}

First attempt was the handoff-planned II-7 optimization: a precomputed \texttt{U\allowbreak{}8\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}M\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}[\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}5\allowbreak{}6\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}*\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}4\allowbreak{}]} table of \texttt{[\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}]} for every byte value 0--255, with \texttt{w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l} short-circuiting to a direct table read for \texttt{n\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}<\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}5\allowbreak{}6}. The reasoning was that the SGR RGB path calls \texttt{w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l} six times per cell × 9168 cells = 55 k calls per frame, and swapping integer division for table reads should shave meaningfully.

Bench said otherwise. 40-sample run:

V8 is already generating near-optimal code for the 2/3-digit arithmetic path --- the \texttt{|\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}0} integer-cast divisions lower to simple imuls, and the per-call cost is already dominated by the function-call and the byte writes themselves, not the arithmetic. Reverted the table; the arithmetic path stays.

\textbf{The win: skip the redundant first-cell compare}

The inner batch loop was doing a redundant \texttt{c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}M\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h} 10-byte compare on its first iteration. The outer loop had already proven \texttt{!\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e} for that exact cell moments before entering the batch; the inner compare at \texttt{b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}X\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}x} recomputes the same value with the same source offsets.

In diff-gradient every batch is one cell (next cell has a different style, so the batch exits immediately). That single iteration does the redundant compare, writes the char, then loops back and breaks on the next iteration's style mismatch. The redundant compare fires 9168 times per frame --- pure waste.

The fix is a small restructure: hoist the first-cell char write out of the loop, because both break conditions are already guaranteed false at the leader position. The loop starts at \texttt{b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}X\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}+\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}1} with a cleaner invariant: \texttt{b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}X\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}>\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}x} is always true, so the style-break branch drops its \texttt{b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}X\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}>\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}x} guard as well.

\begin{CodeBlock}
// Write the first cell unconditionally (known !same + leader style).
off = writeCharBytes(buf, off, tBuf, tOff, tSide);
let batchX = x + 1;

while (batchX < width) {
  const bIdx = y * width + batchX;
  const bOff = bIdx * CELL_STRIDE;
  if (!packedStyleBytesEqual(tBuf, tOff, tBuf, bOff)) break;
  // ... cellsMatch break check, writeCharBytes, batchX++ ...
}
\end{CodeBlock}

\textbf{diff-gradient is officially under the 1.5 ms target.} 1.45 ms p50, 1.42 ms p-min. The paint scenarios drift −2 to −3\% in the same direction but within CoV; they don't touch the inner loop at all, so the paint shift is most likely JIT reshuffling from the smaller hot-path symbol footprint.

The hex-parse paint-side regression (RE-008's theme-heavy paint path) is now paid back to neutral via the theme token color cache (phase A), and the diff path is 32--96 \% faster than it was before RE-008 ever landed. The cycle's primary goal --- recovering from the \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e} regression without losing anything else --- is comfortably met.

\textbf{Files touched (II-5)}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s} --- inner batch loop restructure in \texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d}, comment block explaining the invariant change.
\end{itemize}

(The u8 decimal table from the detour is not in the commit --- it was reverted after measuring no real gain.)

\textbf{Smoke test}

\texttt{n\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d} (landing + docs) passes. All 2807 tests pass. The hoisted first-cell emission produces the same bytes as the old path for every test surface.

\textbf{What Part II still has on the table}

II-6 (pre-encoded side-table UTF-8) and II-7 (decimal lookup table) stay on the deferred list: - II-6 would only pay off on emoji-heavy frames, which none of the current bench scenarios exercise. - II-7 has been measured and produced no real gain on this hardware + V8 combo. Revisit if a profile points back at \texttt{w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l} later.

Part II is now effectively done --- diff-gradient hits its target, every representative scenario is faster than pre-RE017, and no regressions survive. Ready to move into Phase E ship work (III-5 regression bench, III-1..4 DOGFOOD polish, III-6 version bump to 4.3.0).
